# Testing Guide - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#content-area)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#testing-philosophy)

Testing Philosophy
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The best way to test DeFi plugins is with small amounts on real networks. Test networks often have reliability issues and don’t reflect real-world conditions.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#safe-testing-practices)

Safe Testing Practices
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#1-start-small)

1\. Start Small

Always test with minimal amounts first:

*   0.001 ETH for transfers
*   $1-5 worth of tokens for swaps
*   Smallest viable amounts for bridges

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#2-test-on-low-cost-chains-first)

2\. Test on Low-Cost Chains First

Start testing on chains with low transaction fees:

*   Polygon: ~$0.01 per transaction
*   Base: ~$0.05 per transaction
*   Arbitrum: ~$0.10 per transaction

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#3-progressive-testing)

3\. Progressive Testing

    1. Test basic transfers first
    2. Test token transfers
    3. Test swaps with small amounts
    4. Test bridges last (they're most complex)
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#testing-checklist)

Testing Checklist
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#environment-setup)

Environment Setup

    # Use a dedicated test wallet
    EVM_PRIVATE_KEY=test_wallet_private_key
    
    # Start with one chain
    ETHEREUM_PROVIDER_BASE=https://base-mainnet.g.alchemy.com/v2/YOUR_KEY
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#basic-tests)

Basic Tests

1.  **Wallet Connection**
    
        User: What's my wallet address?
        Agent: [Should show your wallet address]
        
    
2.  **Balance Check**
    
        User: What's my balance?
        Agent: [Should show balances across configured chains]
        
    
3.  **Small Transfer**
    
        User: Send 0.001 ETH to [another test address]
        Agent: [Should execute the transfer]
        
    
4.  **Token Transfer**
    
        User: Send 1 USDC to [test address]
        Agent: [Should handle ERC20 transfer]
        
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#swap-testing)

Swap Testing

Test swaps with minimal amounts:

    User: Swap 0.01 ETH for USDC
    Agent: [Should find best route and execute]
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#error-handling)

Error Handling

Test error scenarios:

*   Insufficient balance
*   Invalid addresses
*   Network issues
*   High slippage

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#monitoring-results)

Monitoring Results
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  **Transaction Verification**
    *   Check block explorers (Etherscan, BaseScan, etc.)
    *   Verify transaction status
    *   Confirm balances updated
2.  **Gas Usage**
    *   Monitor gas costs
    *   Ensure reasonable gas estimates
    *   Check for failed transactions

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#common-issues)

Common Issues
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#%E2%80%9Dinsufficient-funds-for-gas%E2%80%9D)

”Insufficient funds for gas”

*   Ensure you have native tokens for gas
*   Each chain needs its native token (ETH, MATIC, etc.)

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#%E2%80%9Dtransaction-underpriced%E2%80%9D)

”Transaction underpriced”

*   RPC may be congested
*   Try alternative RPC endpoints

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#%E2%80%9Dnonce-too-low%E2%80%9D)

”Nonce too low”

*   Previous transaction may be pending
*   Wait for confirmation or reset nonce

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/evm/testing-guide#production-readiness)

Production Readiness
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Before using in production:

1.  Test all intended operations
2.  Verify error handling works
3.  Ensure proper logging
4.  Set appropriate gas limits
5.  Configure slippage tolerances
6.  Test with your expected volumes

YesNo

# Operations Flow - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#content-area)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#overview)

Overview
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Solana plugin processes DeFi operations through this flow:

    User Message → Action Recognition → AI Parameter Extraction → Execution → Response
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#transfer-flow)

Transfer Flow
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#1-user-intent)

1\. User Intent

    User: Send 1 SOL to alice.sol
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#2-action-recognition)

2\. Action Recognition

The plugin identifies transfer keywords (send, transfer, pay).

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#3-parameter-extraction)

3\. Parameter Extraction

AI extracts:

*   Amount: 1
*   Token: SOL
*   Recipient: alice.sol (resolves to address)

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#4-execution-steps)

4\. Execution Steps

*   Resolve .sol domain if needed
*   Check balance
*   Build transaction with priority fee
*   Sign and send
*   Wait for confirmation

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#5-response)

5\. Response

    Agent: Successfully sent 1 SOL to alice.sol
    Transaction: https://solscan.io/tx/[signature]
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#swap-flow)

Swap Flow
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#1-user-intent-2)

1\. User Intent

    User: Swap 10 SOL for USDC
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#2-jupiter-integration)

2\. Jupiter Integration

*   Get quote from Jupiter API
*   Calculate output amount
*   Check price impact

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#3-execution)

3\. Execution

*   Build swap transaction
*   Add priority fees
*   Execute and monitor

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#4-special-cases)

4\. Special Cases

*   “Swap all” - calculates max balance
*   Custom slippage - applies user preference
*   Route selection - optimizes for best price

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#portfolio-flow)

Portfolio Flow
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#1-user-request)

1\. User Request

    User: What's my portfolio worth?
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#2-data-aggregation)

2\. Data Aggregation

*   Fetch SOL balance
*   Get SPL token balances
*   Query prices from Birdeye API

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#3-response-format)

3\. Response Format

    Total Value: $X,XXX.XX (XX.XX SOL)
    
    Token Balances:
    SOL: 10.5 ($850.50)
    USDC: 250.25 ($250.25)
    BONK: 1,000,000 ($45.20)
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#key-features)

Key Features
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#real-time-updates)

Real-Time Updates

*   WebSocket subscriptions for balance changes
*   Automatic portfolio refresh every 2 minutes
*   Instant transaction notifications

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#smart-token-resolution)

Smart Token Resolution

*   Common symbols (USDC, USDT, BONK) auto-resolved
*   .sol domain support
*   Token metadata caching

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#transaction-optimization)

Transaction Optimization

*   Priority fees for faster confirmation
*   Compute unit optimization
*   Automatic retry on failure

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#error-handling)

Error Handling
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#common-errors)

Common Errors

*   **Insufficient Balance**: Pre-checks prevent failed transactions
*   **Token Not Found**: Clear error messages for unknown tokens
*   **Network Issues**: Automatic retry with backoff
*   **High Slippage**: Warns before executing

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/defi-operations-flow#safety-features)

Safety Features

1.  Balance validation before execution
2.  Address verification
3.  Slippage protection
4.  Transaction simulation when possible

Was this page helpful?

YesNo

# Examples - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#content-area)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#configuration)

Configuration
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#character-configuration)

Character Configuration

Add the Solana plugin to your character file:

    // character.ts
    import { type Character } from '@elizaos/core';
    
    export const character: Character = {
      name: 'SolanaAgent',
      plugins: [\
        // Core plugins\
        '@elizaos/plugin-sql',\
        '@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap',\
        \
        // Solana plugin\
        ...(process.env.SOLANA_PRIVATE_KEY?.trim() ? ['@elizaos/plugin-solana'] : []),\
        \
        // Platform plugins\
        ...(process.env.DISCORD_API_TOKEN?.trim() ? ['@elizaos/plugin-discord'] : []),\
      ],
      settings: {
        secrets: {},
      },
      // ... rest of character configuration
    };
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#environment-variables)

Environment Variables

    # Required - Choose one:
    SOLANA_PRIVATE_KEY=your_base58_private_key_here
    # OR for read-only mode:
    SOLANA_PUBLIC_KEY=your_public_key_here
    
    # Optional - Enhanced RPC
    SOLANA_RPC_URL=https://mainnet.helius-rpc.com/?api-key=YOUR_KEY
    HELIUS_API_KEY=your_helius_key
    
    # Optional - Market data
    BIRDEYE_API_KEY=your_birdeye_key
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#usage-examples)

Usage Examples
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#transfer-operations)

Transfer Operations

The agent understands natural language for transfers:

    User: Send 1 SOL to 7xKXtg2CW87d97TXJSDpbD5jBkheTqA83TZRuJosgAsU
    Agent: I'll send 1 SOL to that address right away.
    
    User: Transfer 100 USDC to alice.sol
    Agent: Transferring 100 USDC to alice.sol.
    
    User: Pay bob 50 BONK tokens
    Agent: Sending 50 BONK to bob.
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#swap-operations)

Swap Operations

    User: Swap 10 SOL for USDC
    Agent: I'll swap 10 SOL for USDC using Jupiter.
    
    User: Exchange all my BONK for SOL
    Agent: Swapping all your BONK tokens for SOL.
    
    User: Trade 100 USDC for JTO with 2% slippage
    Agent: Swapping 100 USDC for JTO with 2% slippage tolerance.
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#portfolio-management)

Portfolio Management

    User: What's my wallet balance?
    Agent: [Shows total portfolio value and individual token balances]
    
    User: How much is my portfolio worth?
    Agent: Your total portfolio value is $X,XXX.XX (XX.XX SOL)
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#custom-plugin-integration)

Custom Plugin Integration
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you need to import the plugin directly in a ProjectAgent:

    // index.ts
    import { type ProjectAgent } from '@elizaos/core';
    import solanaPlugin from '@elizaos/plugin-solana';
    import { character } from './character';
    
    export const projectAgent: ProjectAgent = {
      character,
      plugins: [solanaPlugin], // Import custom plugins here
      init: async (runtime) => {
        // Custom initialization if needed
      }
    };
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#common-patterns)

Common Patterns
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#domain-name-resolution)

Domain Name Resolution

The plugin automatically resolves .sol domains:

    User: Send 5 SOL to vitalik.sol
    Agent: Sending 5 SOL to vitalik.sol [resolves to actual address]
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#token-symbol-resolution)

Token Symbol Resolution

Common tokens are automatically recognized:

    User: Send 100 USDC to alice
    Agent: [Recognizes USDC token mint and handles transfer]
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#all-balance-swaps)

All Balance Swaps

    User: Swap all my BONK for USDC
    Agent: [Calculates max balance and executes swap]
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/defi/solana/examples#slippage-control)

Slippage Control

    User: Swap with 0.5% slippage
    Agent: [Sets custom slippage for the swap]

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#cache-structure)

Cache Structure

    interface TimelineCache {
      tweets: Tweet[];
      users: Map<string, TwitterUser>;
      timestamp: number;
      etag?: string;
    }
    
    interface ProcessingState {
      processedTweets: Set<string>;
      lastProcessTime: number;
      interactionCount: number;
      rateLimitStatus: RateLimitInfo;
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#scoring-components)

Scoring Components

    interface ScoringWeights {
      user: number;      // Default: 3
      time: number;      // Default: 2
      relevance: number; // Default: 5
    }
    
    interface TweetScore {
      tweetId: string;
      userScore: number;
      timeScore: number;
      relevanceScore: number;
      totalScore: number;
      factors: {
        isTargetUser: boolean;
        isVerified: boolean;
        followerCount: number;
        hasKeywords: boolean;
        age: number;
      };
    }

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#performance-tracking)

Performance Tracking

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#configuration-impact)

Configuration Impact
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#algorithm-selection)

Algorithm Selection

| Algorithm | Best For | Performance | Quality |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Weighted | Quality interactions | Slower | Higher |
| Latest | High volume | Faster | Lower |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#weight-configuration-effects)

Weight Configuration Effects

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/twitter/timeline-flow#best-practices)

Best Practices
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  **Cache Management**
    *   Implement TTL for timeline cache
    *   Clear processed tweets periodically
    *   Monitor cache hit rates
2.  **Rate Limit Handling**
    *   Track limits per endpoint
    *   Implement request queuing
    *   Use exponential backoff
3.  **Score Tuning**
    *   Monitor interaction quality
    *   Adjust weights based on results
    *   A/B test different configurations
4.  **Error Recovery**
    *   Implement circuit breakers
    *   Log all failures with context
    *   Graceful degradation
5.  **Performance Monitoring**
    *   Track processing times
    *   Monitor API usage
    *   Alert on anomalies

# Cast Flow - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
>

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#content-area)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#farcaster-cast-flow)

Farcaster Cast Flow
====================================================================================================================

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#overview)

Overview
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This document provides a visual and detailed explanation of how the Farcaster plugin processes casts, from initial receipt through evaluation, response generation, and posting.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#cast-processing-pipeline)

Cast Processing Pipeline
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#detailed-flow-stages)

Detailed Flow Stages
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#1-event-reception)

1\. Event Reception

The plugin polls the Neynar API for relevant events and interactions:

    // Neynar API polling for mentions and timeline
    setInterval(async () => {
      const mentions = await neynarClient.fetchMentions({
        fid: agentFid,
        limit: 10
      });
      
      const timeline = await neynarClient.fetchTimeline({
        fid: agentFid,
        type: 'ForYou'
      });
      
      await processEvents(mentions, timeline);
    }, FARCASTER_POLL_INTERVAL * 60000);
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#2-event-classification)

2\. Event Classification

Events are classified and routed to appropriate handlers:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#3-content-analysis)

3\. Content Analysis

Each cast undergoes multi-stage analysis:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#4-response-decision-tree)

4\. Response Decision Tree

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#5-response-generation)

5\. Response Generation

The response generation process:

    async function generateResponse(context: CastContext): Promise<string> {
      // 1. Build conversation history
      const thread = await getThreadContext(context.parentHash);
      
      // 2. Extract key topics
      const topics = extractTopics(context.text);
      
      // 3. Generate appropriate response
      const response = await llm.generate({
        system: character.personality,
        context: thread,
        topics: topics,
        maxLength: 320
      });
      
      // 4. Validate and format
      return formatCast(response);
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#6-cast-composition)

6\. Cast Composition

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#7-cast-publishing-via-neynar)

7\. Cast Publishing via Neynar

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#interaction-patterns)

Interaction Patterns
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#reply-chains)

Reply Chains

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#channel-participation)

Channel Participation

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#rate-limiting-&-throttling)

Rate Limiting & Throttling
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#error-handling-flow)

Error Handling Flow
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#performance-metrics)

Performance Metrics
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#processing-times)

Processing Times

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#throughput-management)

Throughput Management

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#state-management)

State Management
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#monitoring-&-observability)

Monitoring & Observability
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#best-practices)

Best Practices
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  **Efficient Polling**: Use appropriate intervals to balance responsiveness and API rate limits
2.  **Smart Caching**: Cache user profiles and recent casts to reduce Neynar API calls
3.  **Graceful Degradation**: Handle API failures without losing queued responses
4.  **Context Awareness**: Maintain conversation context across reply chains
5.  **Rate Limit Respect**: Implement proper backoff strategies for Neynar API limits

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#debugging-cast-flow)

Debugging Cast Flow
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Enable detailed logging to trace cast processing:

    // Enable debug mode
    process.env.FARCASTER_DEBUG = 'true';
    
    // Log each stage
    runtime.on('farcaster:event', (event) => {
      console.log(`[${event.stage}]`, event.data);
    });
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/farcaster/cast-flow#summary)

Summary
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Farcaster cast flow is designed to be:

*   **Responsive**: Quick reaction to mentions and replies
*   **Intelligent**: Context-aware response generation
*   **Reliable**: Robust error handling and retry logic
*   **Scalable**: Efficient queue management and rate limiting
*   **Observable**: Comprehensive metrics and logging

# Scenario Command - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#content-area)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#usage)

Usage
------------------------------------------------------------------

    elizaos scenario <subcommand> [options]
    

The scenario command provides a comprehensive framework for defining, executing, and evaluating agent behavior through structured test scenarios.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#subcommands)

Subcommands
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Subcommand | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `run` | Execute a single scenario from a YAML file |
| `matrix` | Execute a scenario matrix for parameter exploration |

* * *

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#run)

run
--------------------------------------------------------------

Execute a scenario defined in a YAML file.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#usage-2)

Usage

    elizaos scenario run <filePath> [options]
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#arguments)

Arguments

| Argument | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `<filePath>` | Path to the `.scenario.yaml` file |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#options)

Options

| Option | Description | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `-l, --live` | Run in live mode, ignoring mocks | `false` |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#examples)

Examples

    # Run a scenario
    elizaos scenario run ./tests/greeting.scenario.yaml
    
    # Run in live mode (no mocking)
    elizaos scenario run ./tests/api-test.scenario.yaml --live
    

* * *

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#matrix)

matrix
--------------------------------------------------------------------

Execute a scenario matrix for exploring parameter combinations.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#usage-3)

Usage

    elizaos scenario matrix <configPath> [options]
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#arguments-2)

Arguments

| Argument | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `<configPath>` | Path to the matrix configuration YAML file |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#options-2)

Options

| Option | Description | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `--dry-run` | Show matrix analysis without executing | `false` |
| `--parallel <number>` | Maximum parallel test runs | `1` |
| `--filter <pattern>` | Filter parameter combinations by pattern | \-  |
| `--verbose` | Show detailed progress information | `false` |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#examples-2)

Examples

    # Analyze matrix without executing
    elizaos scenario matrix ./matrix-config.yaml --dry-run
    
    # Execute matrix with parallel runs
    elizaos scenario matrix ./matrix-config.yaml --parallel 4
    
    # Filter specific combinations
    elizaos scenario matrix ./matrix-config.yaml --filter "model=gpt-4"
    
    # Verbose execution
    elizaos scenario matrix ./matrix-config.yaml --verbose
    

* * *

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#scenario-yaml-format)

Scenario YAML Format
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#basic-structure)

Basic Structure

    name: greeting-test
    description: Test agent greeting behavior
    
    setup:
      mocks:
        - type: llm
          response: "Hello! How can I help you today?"
    
    run:
      - action: send_message
        content: "Hello"
        evaluations:
          - type: string_contains
            value: "Hello"
    
    judgment:
      strategy: all_pass
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#scenario-fields)

Scenario Fields

| Field | Description | Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `name` | Scenario name | Yes |
| `description` | Scenario description | No  |
| `plugins` | List of plugins to load | No  |
| `setup` | Setup configuration (mocks, files) | No  |
| `run` | List of execution steps | Yes |
| `judgment` | How to determine pass/fail | No  |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#evaluation-types)

Evaluation Types

| Type | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `string_contains` | Check if output contains a string |
| `regex_match` | Match output against regex pattern |
| `llm_evaluation` | Use LLM to evaluate response quality |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#judgment-strategies)

Judgment Strategies

| Strategy | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `all_pass` | All evaluations must pass |
| `any_pass` | At least one evaluation must pass |

* * *

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#matrix-configuration)

Matrix Configuration
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#basic-structure-2)

Basic Structure

    name: model-comparison
    description: Compare agent behavior across models
    
    base_scenario: ./base.scenario.yaml
    runs_per_combination: 3
    
    matrix:
      - parameter: setup.mocks[0].model
        values:
          - gpt-4
          - gpt-3.5-turbo
          - claude-3-opus
    
      - parameter: run[0].content
        values:
          - "Hello"
          - "Hi there"
          - "Good morning"
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#matrix-fields)

Matrix Fields

| Field | Description | Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `name` | Matrix name | Yes |
| `description` | Matrix description | No  |
| `base_scenario` | Path to base scenario file | Yes |
| `runs_per_combination` | Runs per parameter combination | Yes |
| `matrix` | List of parameter axes | Yes |

* * *

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#output-structure)

Output Structure
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Scenario runs generate output in the `_logs_` directory:

    _logs_/
    ├── run-001-execution-0.json    # Execution result step 0
    ├── run-001-evaluation-0.json   # Evaluation result step 0
    ├── run-001.json                # Centralized run result
    └── matrix-YYYYMMDD-HHMM/       # Matrix run output
        ├── run-001.json
        ├── run-002.json
        └── ...
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#mocking)

Mocking
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Scenarios support mocking for deterministic testing:

    setup:
      mocks:
        - type: llm
          model: gpt-4
          response: "Mocked response"
    
        - type: action
          name: SEND_MESSAGE
          result:
            success: true
            message: "Mocked action result"
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#plugins)

Plugins
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Specify plugins to load for the scenario:

    plugins:
      - @elizaos/plugin-bootstrap
      - @elizaos/plugin-sql
      - name: @elizaos/plugin-discord
        enabled: false  # Disable specific plugin
    

Default plugins (`plugin-sql`, `plugin-bootstrap`, `plugin-openai`) are always loaded.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario#related-commands)

Related Commands
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`report`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/report)
    : Generate reports from scenario output
*   [`test`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/test)
    : Run project tests

Was this page helpful?

YesNo

# elizaOS CLI Overview - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#content-area)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#installation)

Installation
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Install the elizaOS CLI globally using Bun:

    bun install -g @elizaos/cli
    

**Video Tutorial**: [**Full CLI Reference**](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=agI0yOPWBwk&list=PLrjBjP4nU8ehOgKAa0-XddHzE0KK0nNvS&index=8)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#available-commands)

Available Commands
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Command | Description |
| --- | --- |
| [`create`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/create) | Initialize a new project, plugin, or agent |
| [`monorepo`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/monorepo) | Clone elizaOS monorepo from a specific branch (defaults to develop) |
| [`plugins`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/plugins) | Manage elizaOS plugins |
| [`agent`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/agent) | Manage elizaOS agents |
| [`tee`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee) | Manage TEE deployments |
| [`start`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start) | Start the Eliza agent with configurable plugins and services |
| [`update`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update) | Update elizaOS CLI and project dependencies |
| [`test`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/test) | Run tests for Eliza agent projects and plugins |
| [`env`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/env) | Manage environment variables and secrets |
| [`dev`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev) | Start the project or plugin in development mode with auto-rebuild, detailed logging, and file change detection |
| [`publish`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish) | Publish a plugin to the registry |
| [`deploy`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/deploy) | Deploy ElizaOS project to AWS ECS |
| [`login`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/login) | Authenticate with ElizaOS Cloud |
| [`containers`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/containers) | Manage ElizaOS Cloud container deployments |
| [`scenario`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/scenario) | Execute and manage test scenarios |
| [`report`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/report) | Generate reports from scenario matrix runs |

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#global-options)

Global Options
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

These options apply to all commands:

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--help`, `-h` | Display help information |
| `--version`, `-v` | Display version information |
| `--no-emoji` | Disable emoji characters in the output |
| `--no-auto-install` | Disable the automatic prompt to install Bun if not detected |
| `-d`, `--debug` | Enable debug logs (`LOG_LEVEL=debug`) |
| `--verbose` | Enable verbose/trace logs (`LOG_LEVEL=trace`) |
| `-q`, `--quiet` | Only show errors (`LOG_LEVEL=error`) |
| `--log-json` | Output logs in JSON format (useful for log aggregation) |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#logging-levels)

Logging Levels

Control verbosity with these flags (from most to least verbose):

    # Maximum verbosity - trace every operation
    elizaos start --verbose
    
    # Debug mode - detailed debugging info
    elizaos start --debug
    
    # Normal mode (default) - standard output
    elizaos start
    
    # Quiet mode - errors only
    elizaos start --quiet
    

For JSON logging in production environments:

    elizaos start --log-json | jq '.'
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#examples)

Examples
------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#getting-version-information)

Getting Version Information

    # Check your CLI version
    elizaos --version
    
    # Get help for the 'agent' command
    elizaos agent --help
    
    # Get help for the 'agent start' subcommand
    elizaos agent start --help
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#project-structure)

Project Structure
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For detailed information about project and plugin structure, see the [Quickstart Guide](https://docs.elizaos.ai/quickstart)
.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#environment-configuration)

Environment Configuration
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Configure your API keys and environment variables with the `env` command:

    # Edit local environment variables interactively
    elizaos env edit-local
    
    # List all environment variables
    elizaos env list
    
    # Interactive environment manager
    elizaos env interactive
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#development-vs-production)

Development vs Production
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

elizaOS supports two main modes of operation:

Development Mode
----------------

Hot reloading, detailed error messages, and file watching for rapid development.

Production Mode
---------------

Optimized performance and production-ready configuration for deployment.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#quick-start)

Quick Start
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For a complete guide to getting started with elizaOS, see the [Quickstart Guide](https://docs.elizaos.ai/quickstart)
.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#creating-a-new-project)

Creating a new project

    # Create a new project using the interactive wizard
    elizaos create
    
    # Or specify a name directly
    elizaos create my-agent-project
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#starting-a-project)

Starting a project

    # Navigate to your project directory
    cd my-agent-project
    
    # Start the project
    elizaos start
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#development-mode)

Development mode

    # Run in development mode with hot reloading
    elizaos dev
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#working-with-projects)

Working with Projects
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

elizaOS organizes work into projects, which can contain one or more agents along with their configurations, knowledge files, and dependencies. The CLI provides commands to manage the entire lifecycle of a project:

1.  **Create** a new project with `create`
2.  **Configure** settings with `env`
3.  **Develop** using `dev` for hot reloading
4.  **Test** functionality with `test`
5.  **Start** in production with `start`
6.  **Share** by publishing with `publish`

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#working-with-plugins)

Working with Plugins
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Plugins extend the functionality of your agents. Use the `plugins` command for managing plugins and `publish` for publishing your own:

    # List available plugins
    elizaos plugins list
    
    # Add a plugin to your project
    elizaos plugins add @elizaos/plugin-discord
    
    # Publish your plugin (from plugin directory)
    elizaos publish
    
    # Test publishing without making changes
    elizaos publish --test
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/overview#related-documentation)

Related Documentation
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Quickstart Guide
----------------

Complete workflow guide to get started with elizaOS

Environment Configuration
-------------------------

Managing environment variables and configuration

# TEE Command - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#content-area)

The `tee` command provides access to Trusted Execution Environment (TEE) deployment and management capabilities through integrated vendor CLIs.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#overview)

Overview
-------------------------------------------------------------------

TEE (Trusted Execution Environment) enables secure and verifiable agent operations on blockchain. The `tee` command currently supports Phala Cloud as a TEE provider, with the potential for additional vendors in the future.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#installation)

Installation
---------------------------------------------------------------------------

    bun install -g @elizaos/cli
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#command-structure)

Command Structure
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    elizaos tee <vendor> [vendor-specific-commands]
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#available-vendors)

Available Vendors
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#phala-cloud)

Phala Cloud

The `phala` subcommand provides a wrapper for the official Phala Cloud CLI, allowing you to manage TEE deployments on Phala Cloud directly through elizaOS.

    elizaos tee phala [phala-cli-commands]
    

The Phala CLI will be automatically downloaded via bunx if not already installed.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#usage-examples)

Usage Examples
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#get-phala-cli-help)

Get Phala CLI Help

    # Display Phala CLI help
    elizaos tee phala help
    
    # Get help for a specific Phala command
    elizaos tee phala cvms help
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#authentication)

Authentication

    # Login to Phala Cloud with your API key
    elizaos tee phala auth login <api-key>
    
    # Check authentication status
    elizaos tee phala auth status
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#managing-cvms-confidential-virtual-machines)

Managing CVMs (Confidential Virtual Machines)

    # List all CVMs
    elizaos tee phala cvms list
    
    # Create a new CVM
    elizaos tee phala cvms create --name my-agent-app --compose ./docker-compose.yml
    
    # Get CVM details
    elizaos tee phala cvms get <cvm-id>
    
    # Update a CVM
    elizaos tee phala cvms update <cvm-id> --compose ./docker-compose.yml
    
    # Delete a CVM
    elizaos tee phala cvms delete <cvm-id>
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#additional-phala-commands)

Additional Phala Commands

The Phala CLI also provides these additional commands:

    # Docker Registry Management
    elizaos tee phala docker login       # Login to Docker Hub
    elizaos tee phala docker logout      # Logout from Docker Hub
    
    # TEE Simulator (for local testing)
    elizaos tee phala simulator start    # Start local TEE simulator
    elizaos tee phala simulator stop     # Stop local TEE simulator
    elizaos tee phala simulator status   # Check simulator status
    
    # Demo Deployment
    elizaos tee phala demo deploy        # Deploy a demo application to Phala Cloud
    elizaos tee phala demo list          # List deployed demos
    elizaos tee phala demo delete <id>   # Delete a demo deployment
    
    # Account Management
    elizaos tee phala join               # Join Phala Cloud and get a free account
    elizaos tee phala free               # Alias for join - get free CVM credits
    
    # Node Management
    elizaos tee phala nodes list         # List available TEE nodes
    elizaos tee phala nodes get <id>     # Get details about a specific node
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#tee-agent-deployment)

TEE Agent Deployment

For deploying elizaOS agents to TEE environments:

1.  First, create a TEE-compatible project:
    
        elizaos create my-tee-agent --type tee
        
    
2.  Configure your agent and prepare deployment files
3.  Deploy to Phala Cloud:
    
        elizaos tee phala cvms create --name my-tee-agent --compose ./docker-compose.yml
        
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#configuration)

Configuration
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#prerequisites)

Prerequisites

*   Bun installed (required for automatic Phala CLI installation)
*   Phala Cloud account and API key (for deployment operations)
*   Docker compose file for CVM deployments

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#environment-variables)

Environment Variables

When deploying TEE agents, ensure your environment variables are properly configured:

    # Set up your Phala API key
    export PHALA_API_KEY="your-api-key"
    
    # Or add to your .env file
    echo "PHALA_API_KEY=your-api-key" >> .env
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#advanced-usage)

Advanced Usage
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#direct-phala-cli-access)

Direct Phala CLI Access

All Phala CLI commands and options are available through the wrapper:

    # Any Phala CLI command can be used
    elizaos tee phala [any-phala-command] [options]
    

For the complete list of Phala CLI commands and options, run:

    elizaos tee phala help
    

Or visit the official Phala CLI documentation:

    bunx phala help
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#troubleshooting)

Troubleshooting
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#common-issues)

Common Issues

1.  **bunx not found**: Install Bun from [bun.sh](https://bun.sh/)
    :
    
        curl -fsSL https://bun.sh/install | bash
        
    
2.  **Authentication failures**: Ensure your API key is valid and you’re logged in:
    
        elizaos tee phala auth login <api-key>
        
    
3.  **Deployment errors**: Check your docker-compose.yml file is valid and all required services are defined

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#debug-mode)

Debug Mode

For detailed output when troubleshooting:

    # Run with verbose logging
    LOG_LEVEL=debug elizaos tee phala cvms list
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#integration-with-elizaos)

Integration with elizaOS
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TEE deployments enable:

*   **Secure key management**: Private keys never leave the TEE
*   **Verifiable computation**: Cryptographic proof of agent behavior
*   **Blockchain integration**: Direct onchain operations with attestation
*   **Privacy preservation**: Sensitive data processing in secure enclaves

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#related-documentation)

Related Documentation
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [Creating TEE Projects](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/create#tee-trusted-execution-environment)
    
*   [Phala Cloud Documentation](https://docs.phala.network/)
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/tee#security-considerations)

Security Considerations
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When deploying agents to TEE:

1.  Never commit private keys or sensitive configuration
2.  Use environment variables for secrets
3.  Verify attestation reports for production deployments
4.  Follow Phala Cloud security best practices

    interface TelegramMessageState {
      // Core message data

Callback State

Best Practices
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  **Update Handling**
    *   Process updates asynchronously
    *   Implement proper error boundaries
    *   Log all update types
2.  **State Management**
    *   Maintain minimal state
    *   Use TTL for cached data
    *   Clean up old conversations
3.  **Performance**
    *   Batch similar operations
    *   Use webhooks in production
    *   Implement connection pooling
4.  **Error Recovery**
    *   Implement exponential backoff
    *   Log errors with context
    *   Provide fallback responses
5.  **Security**
    *   Validate all inputs
    *   Sanitize user content
    *   Implement rate limiting

# Database Adapters - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#content-area)

The SQL plugin provides two database adapters that extend a common `BaseDrizzleAdapter`:

*   **PGLite Adapter** - Embedded PostgreSQL for development and testing
*   **PostgreSQL Adapter** - Full PostgreSQL for production environments

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#architecture-overview)

Architecture Overview
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Both adapters share the same base functionality through `BaseDrizzleAdapter`, which implements the `IDatabaseAdapter` interface from `@elizaos/core`. The adapters handle:

*   Connection management through dedicated managers
*   Automatic retry logic for database operations
*   Schema introspection and dynamic migrations
*   Embedding dimension configuration (default: 384 dimensions)

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#pglite-adapter)

PGLite Adapter
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `PgliteDatabaseAdapter` uses an embedded PostgreSQL instance that runs entirely in Node.js.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#key-features)

Key Features

*   **Zero external dependencies** - No PostgreSQL installation required
*   **File-based persistence** - Data stored in local filesystem
*   **Singleton connection manager** - Ensures single database instance per process
*   **Automatic initialization** - Database created on first use

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#implementation-details)

Implementation Details

    export class PgliteDatabaseAdapter extends BaseDrizzleAdapter {
      private manager: PGliteClientManager;
      protected embeddingDimension: EmbeddingDimensionColumn = DIMENSION_MAP[384];
    
      constructor(agentId: UUID, manager: PGliteClientManager) {
        super(agentId);
        this.manager = manager;
        this.db = drizzle(this.manager.getConnection());
      }
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#connection-management)

Connection Management

The `PGliteClientManager` handles:

*   Singleton PGLite instance creation
*   Data directory resolution and creation
*   Connection persistence across adapter instances

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#postgresql-adapter)

PostgreSQL Adapter
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `PgDatabaseAdapter` connects to a full PostgreSQL database using connection pooling.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#key-features-2)

Key Features

*   **Connection pooling** - Efficient resource management
*   **Automatic retry logic** - Built-in resilience for transient failures
*   **Production-ready** - Designed for scalable deployments
*   **SSL support** - Secure connections when configured
*   **Cloud compatibility** - Works with Supabase, Neon, and other PostgreSQL providers

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#implementation-details-2)

Implementation Details

    export class PgDatabaseAdapter extends BaseDrizzleAdapter {
      protected embeddingDimension: EmbeddingDimensionColumn = DIMENSION_MAP[384];
      private manager: PostgresConnectionManager;
    
      constructor(agentId: UUID, manager: PostgresConnectionManager, _schema?: any) {
        super(agentId);
        this.manager = manager;
        this.db = manager.getDatabase();
      }
    
      protected async withDatabase<T>(operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
        return await this.withRetry(async () => {
          const client = await this.manager.getClient();
          try {
            const db = drizzle(client);
            this.db = db;
            return await operation();
          } finally {
            client.release();
          }
        });
      }
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#connection-management-2)

Connection Management

The `PostgresConnectionManager` provides:

*   Connection pool management (default size: 20)
*   SSL configuration based on environment
*   Singleton pattern to prevent multiple pools
*   Graceful shutdown handling

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#adapter-selection)

Adapter Selection
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The adapter is automatically selected based on environment configuration:

    export function createDatabaseAdapter(
      config: {
        dataDir?: string;
        postgresUrl?: string;
      },
      agentId: UUID
    ): IDatabaseAdapter {
      if (config.postgresUrl) {
        // PostgreSQL adapter for production
        if (!globalSingletons.postgresConnectionManager) {
          globalSingletons.postgresConnectionManager = new PostgresConnectionManager(
            config.postgresUrl
          );
        }
        return new PgDatabaseAdapter(agentId, globalSingletons.postgresConnectionManager);
      } else {
        // PGLite adapter for development
        const resolvedDataDir = resolvePgliteDir(config.dataDir);
        if (!globalSingletons.pgLiteClientManager) {
          globalSingletons.pgLiteClientManager = new PGliteClientManager(resolvedDataDir);
        }
        return new PgliteDatabaseAdapter(agentId, globalSingletons.pgLiteClientManager);
      }
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#migration-handling)

Migration Handling
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Important**: Both adapters delegate migration handling to the `DatabaseMigrationService`. The adapters themselves do not run migrations directly.

    // In both adapters:
    async runMigrations(): Promise<void> {
      logger.debug('Migrations are handled by the migration service');
      // Migrations are handled by the migration service, not the adapter
    }
    

The migration service handles:

*   Plugin schema discovery and registration
*   Dynamic table creation and updates
*   Schema introspection for existing tables
*   Dependency resolution for table creation order

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#best-practices)

Best Practices
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#development-pglite)

Development (PGLite)

1.  Use default data directory for consistency
2.  Clear data directory between test runs if needed
3.  Be aware of file system limitations
4.  Suitable for single-instance development

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#production-postgresql)

Production (PostgreSQL)

1.  Always use connection pooling
2.  Configure SSL for secure connections
3.  Monitor connection pool usage
4.  Use environment variables for configuration
5.  Implement proper backup strategies

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#configuration)

Configuration
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The SQL plugin automatically selects the appropriate adapter based on environment variables.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#environment-variables)

Environment Variables

    # .env file
    
    # For PostgreSQL (production)
    POSTGRES_URL=postgresql://user:password@host:5432/database
    
    # For custom PGLite directory (optional)
    # If not set, defaults to ./.eliza/.elizadb
    PGLITE_DATA_DIR=/path/to/custom/db
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#configuration-priority)

Configuration Priority

1.  **If `POSTGRES_URL` is set** → Uses PostgreSQL adapter
2.  **If `POSTGRES_URL` is not set** → Uses PGLite adapter
    *   With `PGLITE_DATA_DIR` if specified
    *   Otherwise uses default path: `./.eliza/.elizadb`

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#postgresql-configuration)

PostgreSQL Configuration

The PostgreSQL adapter supports any PostgreSQL-compatible database:

*   **Supabase** - Use the connection string from your project settings
*   **Neon** - Use the connection string from your Neon console
*   **Amazon RDS PostgreSQL**
*   **Google Cloud SQL PostgreSQL**
*   **Self-hosted PostgreSQL** (v12+)

Example connection strings:

    # Supabase
    POSTGRES_URL=postgresql://postgres:[password]@[project].supabase.co:5432/postgres
    
    # Neon
    POSTGRES_URL=postgresql://[user]:[password]@[project].neon.tech/[database]?sslmode=require
    
    # Standard PostgreSQL
    POSTGRES_URL=postgresql://user:password@localhost:5432/mydb
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/database-adapters#error-handling)

Error Handling
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Both adapters include:

*   Automatic retry logic (3 attempts by default)
*   Exponential backoff between retries
*   Detailed error logging
*   Graceful degradation

The adapters handle common scenarios like:

*   Connection timeouts
*   Transient network failures
*   Pool exhaustion (PostgreSQL)
*   File system errors (PGLite)

# Development Mode - ElizaOS Documentation

*   Overview
    
*   Examples
    
*   Features
    
*   Troubleshooting
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#usage)

Usage
-------------------------------------------------------------

    elizaos dev [options]
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#options)

Options
-----------------------------------------------------------------

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `-c, --configure` | Reconfigure services and AI models (skips using saved configuration) |
| `--character [paths...]` | Character file(s) to use - accepts paths or URLs |
| `-b, --build` | Build the project before starting |
| `-p, --port <port>` | Port to listen on (default: 3000) |
| `-h, --help` | Display help for command |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#basic-development-mode)

Basic Development Mode

    # Navigate to your project directory
    cd my-agent-project
    
    # Start development mode
    elizaos dev
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#development-with-configuration)

Development with Configuration

    # Start dev mode with custom port
    elizaos dev --port 8080
    
    # Force reconfiguration of services
    elizaos dev --configure
    
    # Build before starting development
    elizaos dev --build
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#character-file-specification)

Character File Specification

    # Single character file
    elizaos dev --character assistant.json
    
    # Multiple character files (space-separated)
    elizaos dev --character assistant.json chatbot.json
    
    # Multiple character files (comma-separated)
    elizaos dev --character "assistant.json,chatbot.json"
    
    # Character file without extension (auto-adds .json)
    elizaos dev --character assistant
    
    # Load character from URL
    elizaos dev --character https://example.com/characters/assistant.json
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#combined-options)

Combined Options

    # Full development setup
    elizaos dev --port 4000 --character "assistant.json,chatbot.json" --build --configure
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#development-features)

Development Features
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The dev command provides comprehensive development capabilities:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#auto-rebuild-and-restart)

Auto-Rebuild and Restart

*   **File Watching**: Monitors `.ts`, `.js`, `.tsx`, and `.jsx` files for changes
*   **Automatic Rebuilding**: Rebuilds project when source files change
*   **Server Restart**: Automatically restarts the server after successful rebuilds
*   **TypeScript Support**: Compiles TypeScript files during rebuilds

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#project-detection)

Project Detection

*   **Project Mode**: Automatically detects elizaOS projects based on package.json configuration
*   **Plugin Mode**: Detects and handles plugin development appropriately
*   **Monorepo Support**: Builds core packages when working in monorepo context

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#development-workflow)

Development Workflow

1.  Detects whether you’re in a project or plugin directory
2.  Performs initial build (if needed)
3.  Starts the server with specified options
4.  Sets up file watching for source files
5.  Rebuilds and restarts when files change

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#file-watching-behavior)

File Watching Behavior
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#watched-files)

Watched Files

*   TypeScript files (`.ts`, `.tsx`)
*   JavaScript files (`.js`, `.jsx`)

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#watched-directories)

Watched Directories

*   Source directory (`src/`)
*   Project root (if no src directory exists)

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#ignored-paths)

Ignored Paths

*   `node_modules/` directory
*   `dist/` directory
*   `.git/` directory

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#debouncing)

Debouncing

*   Changes are debounced with a 300ms delay to prevent rapid rebuilds
*   Multiple rapid changes trigger only one rebuild cycle

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#project-type-detection)

Project Type Detection
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The dev command uses intelligent project detection:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#plugin-detection)

Plugin Detection

Identifies plugins by checking for:

*   `eliza.type: "plugin"` in package.json
*   Package name containing `plugin-`
*   Keywords: `elizaos-plugin` or `eliza-plugin`

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#project-detection-2)

Project Detection

Identifies projects by checking for:

*   `eliza.type: "project"` in package.json
*   Package name containing `project-` or `-org`
*   Keywords: `elizaos-project` or `eliza-project`
*   `src/index.ts` with Project export

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#monorepo-support)

Monorepo Support
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When running in a monorepo context, the dev command:

1.  **Builds Core Packages**: Automatically builds essential monorepo packages:
    *   `packages/core`
    *   `packages/client`
    *   `packages/plugin-bootstrap`
2.  **Dependency Resolution**: Ensures proper build order for dependencies
3.  **Change Detection**: Monitors both core packages and current project for changes

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#development-logs)

Development Logs
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The dev command provides detailed logging:

    # Project detection
    [info] Running in project mode
    [info] Package name: my-agent-project
    
    # Build process
    [info] Building project...
    [success] Build successful
    
    # Server management
    [info] Starting server...
    [info] Stopping current server process...
    
    # File watching
    [info] Setting up file watching for directory: /path/to/project
    [success] File watching initialized in: /path/to/project/src
    [info] Found 15 TypeScript/JavaScript files in the watched directory
    
    # Change detection
    [info] File event: change - src/index.ts
    [info] Triggering rebuild for file change: src/index.ts
    [info] Rebuilding project after file change...
    [success] Rebuild successful, restarting server...
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#character-file-handling)

Character File Handling
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#supported-formats)

Supported Formats

*   **Local files**: Relative or absolute paths
*   **URLs**: HTTP/HTTPS URLs to character files
*   **Extension optional**: `.json` extension is automatically added if missing

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#multiple-characters)

Multiple Characters

Multiple character files can be specified using:

*   Space separation: `file1.json file2.json`
*   Comma separation: `"file1.json,file2.json"`
*   Mixed format: `"file1.json, file2.json"`

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#troubleshooting)

Troubleshooting
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#build-failures)

Build Failures

    # If initial build fails
    [error] Initial build failed: Error message
    [info] Continuing with dev mode anyway...
    
    # Check for TypeScript errors
    bun i && bun run build
    
    # Try dev mode with explicit build
    elizaos dev --build
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#bun-installation-issues)

Bun Installation Issues

    # If you see "bun: command not found" errors
    # Install Bun using the appropriate command for your system:
    
    # Linux/macOS:
    curl -fsSL https://bun.sh/install | bash
    
    # Windows:
    powershell -c "irm bun.sh/install.ps1 | iex"
    
    # macOS with Homebrew:
    brew install bun
    
    # After installation, restart your terminal or:
    source ~/.bashrc  # Linux
    source ~/.zshrc   # macOS with zsh
    
    # Verify installation:
    bun --version
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#file-watching-issues)

File Watching Issues

    # If file changes aren't detected
    [warn] No directories are being watched! File watching may not be working.
    
    # Check if you're in the right directory
    pwd
    ls src/
    
    # Verify file types being modified (.ts, .js, .tsx, .jsx)
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#server-restart-problems)

Server Restart Problems

    # If server doesn't restart after changes
    [warn] Failed to kill server process, trying force kill...
    
    # Manual restart
    # Press Ctrl+C to stop, then restart:
    elizaos dev
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#port-conflicts)

Port Conflicts

    # If default port is in use
    [error] Port 3000 already in use
    
    # Use different port
    elizaos dev --port 8080
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#configuration-issues)

Configuration Issues

    # If having configuration problems
    elizaos dev --configure
    
    # Check environment setup
    elizaos env list
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev#related-commands)

Related Commands
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`start`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start)
    : Start your project in production mode
*   [`test`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/test)
    : Run tests for your project
*   [`env`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/env)
    : Configure environment variables for development
*   [`create`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/create)
    : Create new projects with development structure
