# Start Command - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#content-area)

*   Overview
    
*   Examples
    
*   Features
    
*   Troubleshooting
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#usage)

Usage
---------------------------------------------------------------

    elizaos start [options]
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#options)

Options
-------------------------------------------------------------------

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `-c, --configure` | Reconfigure services and AI models |
| `--character <paths...>` | Character file(s) to use |
| `-p, --port <port>` | Port to listen on |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#basic-usage)

Basic Usage

    # Start with default configuration
    elizaos start
    
    # Start on custom port
    elizaos start --port 8080
    
    # Force reconfiguration
    elizaos start --configure
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#character-configuration)

Character Configuration

    # Start with single character file
    elizaos start --character ./character.json
    
    # Start with multiple character files
    elizaos start --character ./char1.json ./char2.json
    
    # Mix local files and URLs
    elizaos start --character ./local.json https://example.com/remote.json
    
    # Character files without .json extension
    elizaos start --character assistant support-bot
    
    # Comma-separated format also works
    elizaos start --character "char1.json,char2.json"
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#advanced-configurations)

Advanced Configurations

    # Reconfigure services before starting
    elizaos start --configure
    
    # Start with specific character on custom port
    elizaos start --character ./my-bot.json --port 4000
    
    # Complete setup for production deployment
    elizaos start --character ./production-bot.json --port 3000
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#production-deployment)

Production Deployment

    # With environment file
    cp .env.production .env
    elizaos start
    
    # Background process (Linux/macOS)
    nohup elizaos start > elizaos.log 2>&1 &
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#health-checks)

Health Checks

    # Verify service is running
    curl http://localhost:3000/health
    
    # Check process status
    ps aux | grep elizaos
    
    # Monitor logs
    tail -f elizaos.log
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#production-features)

Production Features
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When you run `start`, elizaOS provides production-ready features:

1.  **Optimized Performance**: Runs with production optimizations
2.  **Stable Configuration**: Uses saved configuration by default
3.  **Service Management**: Handles service connections and disconnections
4.  **Error Recovery**: Automatic recovery from transient errors
5.  **Resource Management**: Efficient resource allocation and cleanup

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#startup-process)

Startup Process
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When you run the `start` command, elizaOS:

1.  **Project Detection**: Detects whether you’re in a project or plugin directory
2.  **Configuration Loading**: Loads and validates the configuration
3.  **Database Initialization**: Initializes the database system
4.  **Plugin Loading**: Loads required plugins
5.  **Service Startup**: Starts any configured services
6.  **Knowledge Processing**: Processes knowledge files if present
7.  **API Server**: Starts the HTTP API server
8.  **Agent Runtime**: Initializes agent runtimes
9.  **Event Listening**: Begins listening for messages and events

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#project-detection)

Project Detection
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

elizaOS automatically detects the type of directory you’re in and adjusts its behavior accordingly:

*   **elizaOS Projects**: Loads project configuration and starts defined agents
*   **elizaOS Plugins**: Runs in plugin test mode with the default character
*   **Other Directories**: Uses the default Eliza character

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#configuration-management)

Configuration Management
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#default-configuration)

Default Configuration

*   Uses saved configuration from previous runs
*   Loads environment variables from `.env` file
*   Applies project-specific settings

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#force-reconfiguration)

Force Reconfiguration

    # Bypass saved configuration and reconfigure all services
    elizaos start --configure
    

This is useful when:

*   You’ve changed API keys or service credentials
*   You want to select different AI models
*   Service configurations have changed
*   Troubleshooting configuration issues

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#environment-variables)

Environment Variables
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `start` command automatically loads environment variables:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#from-env-file)

From .env File

    # elizaOS looks for .env in the project directory
    cd my-project
    elizaos start  # Loads from ./my-project/.env
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#direct-environment-variables)

Direct Environment Variables

    # Set variables directly
    OPENAI_API_KEY=your-key elizaos start
    
    # Multiple variables
    OPENAI_API_KEY=key1 DISCORD_TOKEN=token1 elizaos start
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#error-handling)

Error Handling
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#character-loading-errors)

Character Loading Errors

If character files fail to load, elizaOS will:

1.  **Log Errors**: Display detailed error messages for each failed character
2.  **Continue Starting**: Use any successfully loaded characters
3.  **Fallback**: Use the default Eliza character if no characters load successfully

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#service-connection-errors)

Service Connection Errors

*   Automatic retry for transient connection issues
*   Graceful degradation when optional services are unavailable
*   Error logging with recovery suggestions

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#port-management)

Port Management
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#default-port)

Default Port

*   Port must be specified with `-p` or `--port` option
*   Automatically detects if port is in use
*   Suggests alternative ports if specified port is unavailable

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#custom-port)

Custom Port

    # Specify custom port
    elizaos start --port 8080
    
    # Check if port is available first
    netstat -an | grep :8080
    elizaos start --port 8080
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#build-process)

Build Process
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `start` command does not include built-in build functionality. To build your project before starting:

    # Build separately before starting
    bun run build
    elizaos start
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#health-checks-2)

Health Checks
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    # Verify service is running
    curl http://localhost:3000/health
    
    # Check process status
    ps aux | grep elizaos
    
    # Monitor logs
    tail -f elizaos.log
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#troubleshooting)

Troubleshooting
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#startup-failures)

Startup Failures

    # Check if another instance is running
    ps aux | grep elizaos
    pkill -f elizaos
    
    # Clear any conflicting processes
    # Press Ctrl+C in the terminal where elizaos start is running
    elizaos start
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#port-conflicts)

Port Conflicts

    # Check what's using the port
    lsof -i :3000
    
    # Use different port
    elizaos start --port 3001
    
    # Or stop conflicting service
    sudo kill -9 $(lsof -ti:3000)
    elizaos start
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#character-loading-issues)

Character Loading Issues

    # Verify character file exists and is valid JSON
    cat ./character.json | jq .
    
    # Test with absolute path
    elizaos start --character /full/path/to/character.json
    
    # Start without character to use default
    elizaos start
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#configuration-problems)

Configuration Problems

    # Force reconfiguration to fix corrupted settings
    elizaos start --configure
    
    # Check environment variables
    elizaos env list
    
    # Reset environment if needed
    elizaos env reset
    elizaos start --configure
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#build-failures)

Build Failures

    # Build separately and check for errors
    bun run build
    
    # If build succeeds, then start
    elizaos start
    
    # Install dependencies if missing
    bun install
    bun run build
    elizaos start
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#service-connection-issues)

Service Connection Issues

    # Check internet connectivity
    ping google.com
    
    # Verify API keys are set
    elizaos env list
    
    # Test with minimal configuration
    elizaos start --configure
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start#related-commands)

Related Commands
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`create`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/create)
    : Create a new project to start
*   [`dev`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev)
    : Run in development mode with hot reloading
*   [`agent`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/agent)
    : Manage individual agents
*   [`env`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/env)
    : Configure environment variables

# Knowledge & RAG System - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#content-area)

The Knowledge Plugin is elizaOS’s core RAG system, providing intelligent document management and retrieval capabilities. It enables agents to maintain long-term memory, answer questions from uploaded documents, and learn from conversations.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#key-features)

Key Features
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Zero Configuration
------------------

Works out of the box with sensible defaults

Multiple Formats
----------------

Supports PDF, TXT, MD, DOCX, CSV, and more

Intelligent Processing
----------------------

Smart chunking and contextual embeddings

Cost Optimization
-----------------

90% cost reduction with caching

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#quick-links)

Quick Links
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Quick Start
-----------

Get up and running in 5 minutes

Configuration
-------------

Essential settings and options

Complete Reference
------------------

Comprehensive technical documentation

Examples
--------

Recipes and code samples

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#what-is-the-knowledge-plugin)

What is the Knowledge Plugin?
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Knowledge Plugin transforms your elizaOS agent into an intelligent knowledge base that can:

*   **Store and retrieve documents** in multiple formats
*   **Answer questions** using semantic search
*   **Learn from conversations** automatically
*   **Process web content** via URL ingestion
*   **Manage documents** through a built-in web interface

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#core-capabilities)

Core Capabilities
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#document-processing)

Document Processing

*   Automatic text extraction from PDFs, Word docs, and more
*   Smart chunking with configurable overlap
*   Content-based deduplication
*   Metadata preservation and enrichment

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#retrieval-&-rag)

Retrieval & RAG

*   Semantic search with vector embeddings
*   Automatic context injection into conversations
*   Relevance scoring and ranking
*   Multi-modal retrieval support

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#management-interface)

Management Interface

*   Web-based document browser
*   Upload, view, and delete documents
*   Search and filter capabilities
*   Real-time processing status

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#installation)

Installation
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   elizaos
    
*   bun
    

    elizaos plugins add @elizaos/plugin-knowledge
    

    bun add @elizaos/plugin-knowledge
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#supported-file-types)

Supported File Types
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Documents
---------

PDF, DOCX, TXT, MD

Data
----

CSV, JSON, XML

Web
---

URLs, HTML

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge#advanced-features)

Advanced Features
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Architecture & Flow
-------------------

Understand the internal workings

Contextual Embeddings
---------------------

50% better retrieval accuracy

Testing Guide
-------------

Test your knowledge base

API Reference
-------------

REST endpoints and TypeScript interfaces

Follow the Quick Start

Set up your first knowledge-enabled agent in minutes

# Quick Start Guide - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#content-area)

Give your AI agent the ability to learn from documents and answer questions based on that knowledge. Works out of the box with zero configuration!

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#getting-started-beginner-friendly)

Getting Started (Beginner-Friendly)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#step-1-add-the-plugin)

Step 1: Add the Plugin

The Knowledge plugin works automatically with any elizaOS agent. Just add it to your agent’s plugin list:

    // In your character file (e.g., character.ts)
    export const character = {
      name: 'MyAgent',
      plugins: [\
        '@elizaos/plugin-openai', // ← Make sure you have this\
        '@elizaos/plugin-knowledge', // ← Add this line\
        // ... your other plugins\
      ],
      // ... rest of your character config
    };
    

**That’s it!** Your agent can now learn from documents. You’ll need an `OPENAI_API_KEY` in your `.env` file for embeddings.

Add `OPENAI_API_KEY=your-api-key` to your `.env` file. This is used for creating document embeddings, even if you’re using a different AI provider for chat.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#step-2-upload-documents-optional)

Step 2: Upload Documents (Optional)

Want your agent to automatically learn from documents when it starts?

1.  **Create a `docs` folder** in your project root:
    
        your-project/
        ├── .env
        ├── docs/           ← Create this folder
        │   ├── guide.pdf
        │   ├── manual.txt
        │   └── notes.md
        └── package.json
        
    
2.  **Add this line to your `.env` file:**
    
        LOAD_DOCS_ON_STARTUP=true
        
    
3.  **Start your agent** - it will automatically learn from all documents in the `docs` folder!

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#step-3-ask-questions)

Step 3: Ask Questions

Once documents are loaded, just talk to your agent naturally:

*   “What does the guide say about setup?”
*   “Search your knowledge for configuration info”
*   “What do you know about \[any topic\]?”

Your agent will search through all loaded documents and give you relevant answers!

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#supported-file-types)

Supported File Types
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The plugin can read almost any document:

*   **Text Files:** `.txt`, `.md`, `.csv`, `.json`, `.xml`, `.yaml`
*   **Documents:** `.pdf`, `.doc`, `.docx`
*   **Code Files:** `.js`, `.ts`, `.py`, `.java`, `.cpp`, `.html`, `.css` and many more

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#using-the-web-interface)

Using the Web Interface
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Knowledge Plugin includes a powerful web interface for managing your agent’s knowledge base.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#accessing-the-knowledge-manager)

Accessing the Knowledge Manager

1.  **Start your agent:**
    
        elizaos start
        
    
2.  **Open your browser** and go to `http://localhost:3000`
3.  **Select your agent** from the list (e.g., “Eliza”)
4.  **Click the Knowledge tab** in the right panel

That’s it! You can now:

*   Upload new documents
*   Search existing documents
*   Delete documents you no longer need
*   See all documents your agent has learned from

You can also drag and drop files directly onto the Knowledge tab to upload them!

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#agent-actions)

Agent Actions
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Your agent automatically gets these new abilities:

*   **PROCESS\_KNOWLEDGE** - “Remember this document: \[file path or text\]”
*   **SEARCH\_KNOWLEDGE** - “Search your knowledge for \[topic\]“

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#examples-in-chat)

Examples in Chat

**First, upload a document through the GUI:**

1.  Go to `http://localhost:3000`
2.  Click on your agent and open the Knowledge tab
3.  Upload a document (e.g., `company_q3_earnings.pdf`)

**Then ask your agent about it:**

    You: What were the Q3 revenue figures?
    Agent: Based on the Q3 earnings report in my knowledge base, the revenue was $2.3M, 
           representing a 15% increase from Q2...
    
    You: Search your knowledge for information about profit margins
    Agent: I found relevant information about profit margins: The Q3 report shows gross 
           margins improved to 42%, up from 38% in the previous quarter...
    
    You: What does the report say about future projections?
    Agent: According to the earnings report, the company projects Q4 revenue to reach 
           $2.8M with continued margin expansion...
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#organizing-your-documents)

Organizing Your Documents
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Create subfolders for better organization:

    docs/
    ├── products/
    │   ├── product-guide.pdf
    │   └── pricing.md
    ├── support/
    │   ├── faqs.txt
    │   └── troubleshooting.md
    └── policies/
        └── terms.pdf
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#basic-configuration-optional)

Basic Configuration (Optional)
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#custom-document-folder)

Custom Document Folder

If you want to use a different folder for documents:

.env

    # Custom path to your documents
    KNOWLEDGE_PATH=/path/to/your/documents
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#provider-settings)

Provider Settings

The plugin automatically uses your existing AI provider. If you’re using OpenRouter:

    // In your character file (e.g., character.ts)
    export const character = {
      name: 'MyAgent',
      plugins: [\
        '@elizaos/plugin-openrouter',\
        '@elizaos/plugin-knowledge', // ← Add this line\
        // ... your other plugins\
      ],
      // ... rest of your character config
    };
    

.env

    OPENROUTER_API_KEY=your-openrouter-api-key
    OPENROUTER_EMBEDDING_MODEL=openai/text-embedding-3-large  # Optional: specify embedding model
    

OpenRouter now supports embeddings natively with multiple models: `text-embedding-3-large`, `qwen3-embedding`, `gemini-embedding`, `mistral-embed`.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#faq)

FAQ
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Q: Do I need any API keys?**  
A: For simple setup, only OPENAI\_API\_KEY. **Q: What if I don’t have any AI plugins?**  
A: You need at least one AI provider plugin (like `@elizaos/plugin-openai`) for embeddings. **Q: Can I upload documents while the agent is running?**  
A: Yes! Use the web interface or just tell your agent to process a file. **Q: How much does this cost?** A: Only the cost of generating embeddings (usually pennies per document). **Q: Where are my documents stored?**  
A: Documents are processed and stored in your agent’s database as searchable chunks.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#common-issues)

Common Issues
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#documents-not-loading)

Documents Not Loading

Make sure:

*   Your `docs` folder exists in the right location
*   `LOAD_DOCS_ON_STARTUP=true` is in your `.env` file
*   Files are in supported formats

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#can%E2%80%99t-access-web-interface)

Can’t Access Web Interface

Check that:

*   Your agent is running (`elizaos start`)
*   You’re using the correct URL: `http://localhost:3000`
*   No other application is using port 3000

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#agent-can%E2%80%99t-find-information)

Agent Can’t Find Information

Try:

*   Using simpler search terms
*   Checking if the document was successfully processed
*   Looking in the Knowledge tab to verify the document is there

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/knowledge/quick-start#next-steps)

Next Steps
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Now that you have the basics working:

*   Try uploading different types of documents
*   Organize your documents into folders
*   Ask your agent complex questions about the content
*   Explore the web interface features

Examples
--------

See the plugin in action

Advanced Guide
--------------

Advanced configuration options

The Knowledge Plugin is designed to work out-of-the-box. You only need to adjust settings if you have specific requirements.

# Event Flow - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#content-area)

This document provides a comprehensive breakdown of how events flow through the Discord plugin system.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#complete-event-flow-diagram)

Complete Event Flow Diagram
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#detailed-event-flows)

Detailed Event Flows
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#1-message-processing-flow)

1\. Message Processing Flow

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#2-voice-channel-flow)

2\. Voice Channel Flow

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#3-slash-command-flow)

3\. Slash Command Flow

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#4-attachment-processing-flow)

4\. Attachment Processing Flow

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#5-multi-server-event-flow)

5\. Multi-Server Event Flow

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#event-type-reference)

Event Type Reference
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#discord-js-events)

Discord.js Events

| Event | Description | Plugin Handler |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ready` | Client is ready | Initialize services |
| `messageCreate` | New message | MessageManager |
| `messageUpdate` | Message edited | MessageManager |
| `messageDelete` | Message deleted | Cleanup handler |
| `interactionCreate` | Slash command/button | Interaction router |
| `guildCreate` | Bot joins server | Server initializer |
| `guildDelete` | Bot leaves server | Cleanup handler |
| `guildMemberAdd` | Member joins | Relationship manager |
| `voiceStateUpdate` | Voice state change | VoiceManager |
| `error` | Client error | Error handler |
| `disconnect` | Lost connection | Reconnection handler |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#elizaos-events-emitted)

elizaOS Events Emitted

| Event | When Emitted | Payload |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WORLD_JOINED` | Bot joins server | World, rooms, entities |
| `MESSAGE_RECEIVED` | Message processed | elizaOS message format |
| `VOICE_MESSAGE_RECEIVED` | Voice transcribed | Transcribed message |
| `REACTION_RECEIVED` | Reaction added | Reaction details |
| `INTERACTION_RECEIVED` | Slash command used | Interaction data |

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#state-management)

State Management
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#message-context)

Message Context

    interface MessageContext {
      channelId: string;
      serverId: string;
      userId: string;
      threadId?: string;
      referencedMessageId?: string;
      attachments: ProcessedAttachment[];
      discordMetadata: {
        messageId: string;
        timestamp: number;
        editedTimestamp?: number;
        isPinned: boolean;
        mentions: string[];
      };
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#voice-context)

Voice Context

    interface VoiceContext {
      channelId: string;
      serverId: string;
      connection: VoiceConnection;
      activeUsers: Map<string, VoiceUserState>;
      recordingState: {
        isRecording: boolean;
        startTime?: number;
        audioBuffer: Buffer[];
      };
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#error-handling-in-event-flow)

Error Handling in Event Flow
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#error-propagation)

Error Propagation

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#performance-considerations)

Performance Considerations
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#event-batching)

Event Batching

For high-volume servers, events are batched:

    class EventBatcher {
      private messageQueue: DiscordMessage[] = [];
      private batchTimer?: NodeJS.Timeout;
      
      addMessage(message: DiscordMessage) {
        this.messageQueue.push(message);
        
        if (!this.batchTimer) {
          this.batchTimer = setTimeout(() => {
            this.processBatch();
          }, 100); // 100ms batch window
        }
      }
      
      private async processBatch() {
        const batch = [...this.messageQueue];
        this.messageQueue = [];
        this.batchTimer = undefined;
        
        // Process messages in parallel
        await Promise.all(
          batch.map(msg => this.processMessage(msg))
        );
      }
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#connection-pooling)

Connection Pooling

Voice connections are pooled to reduce overhead:

    class VoiceConnectionPool {
      private connections = new Map<string, VoiceConnection>();
      private maxConnections = 10;
      
      async getConnection(channelId: string): Promise<VoiceConnection> {
        // Reuse existing connection
        const existing = this.connections.get(channelId);
        if (existing?.state.status === VoiceConnectionStatus.Ready) {
          return existing;
        }
        
        // Check pool limit
        if (this.connections.size >= this.maxConnections) {
          await this.evictOldestConnection();
        }
        
        // Create new connection
        const connection = await this.createConnection(channelId);
        this.connections.set(channelId, connection);
        return connection;
      }
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#monitoring-event-flow)

Monitoring Event Flow
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#event-metrics)

Event Metrics

Track event processing metrics:

    interface EventMetrics {
      eventType: string;
      processingTime: number;
      success: boolean;
      errorType?: string;
      serverId: string;
      channelId: string;
    }
    
    class EventMonitor {
      private metrics: EventMetrics[] = [];
      
      recordEvent(metric: EventMetrics) {
        this.metrics.push(metric);
        
        // Log slow events
        if (metric.processingTime > 1000) {
          logger.warn(`Slow event processing: ${metric.eventType} took ${metric.processingTime}ms`);
        }
      }
      
      getStats() {
        return {
          totalEvents: this.metrics.length,
          averageProcessingTime: this.calculateAverage(),
          errorRate: this.calculateErrorRate(),
          eventBreakdown: this.getEventTypeBreakdown()
        };
      }
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/platform/discord/event-flow#best-practices)

Best Practices
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  **Event Debouncing**
    *   Debounce rapid events (typing indicators, voice state)
    *   Batch similar events when possible
2.  **Error Isolation**
    *   Don’t let one event error affect others
    *   Use try-catch at event handler level
3.  **Resource Management**
    *   Clean up event listeners on disconnect
    *   Limit concurrent event processing
4.  **Monitoring**
    *   Track event processing times
    *   Monitor error rates by event type
    *   Alert on unusual patterns

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/media/upload-media-for-agent#content-area)

Upload media for agent

cURL

    curl --request POST \
      --url http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media \
      --header 'Content-Type: multipart/form-data' \
      --form file='@example-file'

    import requests
    
    url = "http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media"
    
    files = { "file": ("example-file", open("example-file", "rb")) }
    
    response = requests.post(url, files=files)
    
    print(response.text)

    const form = new FormData();
    form.append('file', '<string>');
    
    const options = {method: 'POST'};
    
    options.body = form;
    
    fetch('http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media', options)
      .then(res => res.json())
      .then(res => console.log(res))
      .catch(err => console.error(err));

    <?php
    
    $curl = curl_init();
    
    curl_setopt_array($curl, [\
      CURLOPT_PORT => "3000",\
      CURLOPT_URL => "http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media",\
      CURLOPT_RETURNTRANSFER => true,\
      CURLOPT_ENCODING => "",\
      CURLOPT_MAXREDIRS => 10,\
      CURLOPT_TIMEOUT => 30,\
      CURLOPT_HTTP_VERSION => CURL_HTTP_VERSION_1_1,\
      CURLOPT_CUSTOMREQUEST => "POST",\
      CURLOPT_POSTFIELDS => "-----011000010111000001101001\r\nContent-Disposition: form-data; name=\"file\"; filename=\"example-file\"\r\nContent-Type: application/octet-stream\r\n\r\n<string>\r\n-----011000010111000001101001--",\
      CURLOPT_HTTPHEADER => [\
        "Content-Type: multipart/form-data"\
      ],\
    ]);
    
    $response = curl_exec($curl);
    $err = curl_error($curl);
    
    curl_close($curl);
    
    if ($err) {
      echo "cURL Error #:" . $err;
    } else {
      echo $response;
    }

    package main
    
    import (
    	"fmt"
    	"strings"
    	"net/http"
    	"io"
    )
    
    func main() {
    
    	url := "http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media"
    
    	payload := strings.NewReader("-----011000010111000001101001\r\nContent-Disposition: form-data; name=\"file\"; filename=\"example-file\"\r\nContent-Type: application/octet-stream\r\n\r\n<string>\r\n-----011000010111000001101001--")
    
    	req, _ := http.NewRequest("POST", url, payload)
    
    	res, _ := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
    
    	defer res.Body.Close()
    	body, _ := io.ReadAll(res.Body)
    
    	fmt.Println(string(body))
    
    }

    HttpResponse<String> response = Unirest.post("http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media")
      .body("-----011000010111000001101001\r\nContent-Disposition: form-data; name=\"file\"; filename=\"example-file\"\r\nContent-Type: application/octet-stream\r\n\r\n<string>\r\n-----011000010111000001101001--")
      .asString();

    require 'uri'
    require 'net/http'
    
    url = URI("http://localhost:3000/api/media/{agentId}/upload-media")
    
    http = Net::HTTP.new(url.host, url.port)
    
    request = Net::HTTP::Post.new(url)
    request.body = "-----011000010111000001101001\r\nContent-Disposition: form-data; name=\"file\"; filename=\"example-file\"\r\nContent-Type: application/octet-stream\r\n\r\n<string>\r\n-----011000010111000001101001--"
    
    response = http.request(request)
    puts response.read_body

# Publish Command - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#content-area)

The `elizaos publish` command is the all-in-one tool for releasing your plugin. It handles packaging, publishing to npm, creating a source repository, and submitting your plugin to the official elizaOS registry for discovery.

*   Overview
    
*   Examples
    
*   Publishing Process
    
*   Requirements
    
*   Troubleshooting
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#what-it-does)

What It Does
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `publish` command automates the entire release process:

*   **Validates Your Plugin:** Checks your `package.json` and directory structure against registry requirements
*   **Publishes Your Package:** Pushes your plugin to npm
*   **Creates GitHub Repository:** Initializes a public GitHub repository for your plugin’s source code
*   **Submits to Registry:** Opens a Pull Request to the official [elizaOS Plugin Registry](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry)
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#usage)

Usage
-----------------------------------------------------------------

    elizaos publish [options]
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#options)

Options
---------------------------------------------------------------------

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `--npm` | Publish to npm only (skip GitHub and registry) |
| `-t, --test` | Test publish process without making changes |
| `-d, --dry-run` | Generate registry files locally without publishing |
| `--skip-registry` | Skip publishing to the registry |

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#standard-publishing)

Standard Publishing
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This is the most common workflow. It publishes your package to npm, creates a GitHub repository, and opens a PR to the registry.

    # Navigate to your plugin's root directory
    cd my-awesome-plugin
    
    # Publish to npm and the registry
    elizaos publish
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#testing-and-dry-runs)

Testing and Dry Runs
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use these options to validate your plugin before a real publish.

    # Simulate the entire publish process without making changes
    # Great for checking authentication and validation rules
    elizaos publish --test
    
    # Generate registry submission files locally for inspection
    elizaos publish --dry-run
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#advanced-publishing)

Advanced Publishing
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use these for specific scenarios, like managing a private plugin or handling the registry submission manually.

    # Publish to npm but do not open a PR to the registry
    elizaos publish --skip-registry
    
    # Test npm-only publishing (skip GitHub and registry)
    elizaos publish --test --npm
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#development-lifecycle)

Development Lifecycle
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A typical journey from creation to publishing:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#1-create-&-develop)

1\. Create & Develop

    # Create a new plugin from the template
    elizaos create -t plugin my-awesome-plugin
    cd my-awesome-plugin
    
    # Install dependencies and start development
    bun install
    elizaos dev
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#2-test-&-validate)

2\. Test & Validate

    # Run your plugin's tests
    elizaos test
    
    # Simulate publish to catch issues early
    elizaos publish --test
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#3-publish)

3\. Publish

    # Ensure you're logged into npm
    bunx npm login
    
    # Publish your plugin
    elizaos publish
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#process-steps)

Process Steps
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When you run `elizaos publish`, the CLI performs these actions:

1.  **Validation:** Checks CLI version, plugin structure, and `package.json`
2.  **Authentication:** Prompts for npm and GitHub credentials if needed
3.  **Build:** Compiles TypeScript by running `bun run build`
4.  **Publish Package:** Pushes to npm
5.  **Create GitHub Repo:** Creates public repository (if it doesn’t exist)
6.  **Submit to Registry:** Opens a Pull Request for discovery

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#post-publishing-updates)

Post-Publishing Updates
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `elizaos publish` command is for **initial release only**. Use standard tools for updates.

For subsequent updates:

    # Bump version in package.json
    bun version patch  # or minor/major
    
    # Push new version to npm
    bun publish
    
    # Push code and tags to GitHub
    git push && git push --tags
    

The elizaOS registry automatically detects new npm versions.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#authentication)

Authentication
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#npm-authentication)

npm Authentication

You must be logged in to npm:

    bunx npm login
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#github-authentication)

GitHub Authentication

A Personal Access Token (PAT) is required. You can either:

1.  Set environment variable: `export GITHUB_TOKEN=your_pat_here`
2.  Enter when prompted by the CLI

Required PAT scopes: `repo`, `read:org`, `workflow`

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#plugin-structure)

Plugin Structure
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The CLI validates these requirements before publishing:

| Requirement | Description | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Plugin Name** | Must start with `plugin-` | Auto-checked |
| **Images Directory** | Must have `images/` directory | Auto-created |
| **Logo Image** | `images/logo.jpg` (400x400px, max 500KB) | Manual |
| **Banner Image** | `images/banner.jpg` (1280x640px, max 1MB) | Manual |
| **Description** | Meaningful description | Prompted |
| **Repository URL** | Format: `github:username/repo` | Auto-fixed |
| **agentConfig** | Required in package.json | Auto-fixed |

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#sample-package-json)

Sample package.json
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    {
      "name": "plugin-example",
      "version": "1.0.0",
              "description": "An example elizaOS plugin that demonstrates best practices",
      "main": "dist/index.js",
      "types": "dist/index.d.ts",
      "author": "Your Name <your.email@example.com>",
      "license": "MIT",
      "repository": "github:yourusername/plugin-example",
      "keywords": ["elizaos-plugin", "eliza-plugin"],
      "scripts": {
        "build": "tsc",
        "test": "vitest",
        "dev": "tsc --watch"
      },
      "dependencies": {
        "@elizaos/core": "^1.0.0"
      },
      "devDependencies": {
        "typescript": "^5.0.0",
        "vitest": "^1.0.0"
      },
      "agentConfig": {
        "actions": ["exampleAction"],
        "providers": ["exampleProvider"],
        "evaluators": ["exampleEvaluator"],
        "models": ["gpt-4", "gpt-3.5-turbo"],
        "services": ["discord", "telegram"]
      }
    }
    

The `agentConfig` section tells elizaOS agents how to load your plugin.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#authentication-errors)

Authentication Errors
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#npm-login-issues)

npm Login Issues

    # Refresh credentials
    bunx npm logout
    bunx npm login
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#github-token-issues)

GitHub Token Issues

Generate a new PAT with `repo`, `read:org`, and `workflow` scopes:

    # Set token
    export GITHUB_TOKEN=your_new_token
    
    # Or enter when prompted
    elizaos publish
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#validation-failures)

Validation Failures
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use `--test` to identify issues:

    elizaos publish --test
    

Common problems:

*   Plugin name doesn’t start with `plugin-`
*   Missing or incorrectly sized images
*   Invalid repository URL format

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#build-failures)

Build Failures
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Debug TypeScript errors:

    # Ensure dependencies are installed
    bun install
    
    # Run build manually
    bun run build
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#version-conflicts)

Version Conflicts
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Cannot publish over existing versions:

    # Check current version
    bunx npm view your-plugin version
    
    # Bump version
    bun version patch
    
    # Retry
    elizaos publish
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#github-repository-exists)

GitHub Repository Exists
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If repository already exists:

    # Verify it's correct
    gh repo view yourusername/plugin-name
    
    # Publish to npm only (skip GitHub and registry)
    elizaos publish --npm
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#registry-submission-issues)

Registry Submission Issues
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    # Test registry generation
    elizaos publish --dry-run
    
    # Check generated files
    ls packages/registry/
    
    # Skip registry if needed
    elizaos publish --skip-registry
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#ci/cd-integration)

CI/CD Integration
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Example GitHub Actions workflow:

    name: Publish
    on:
      release:
        types: [created]
    
    jobs:
      publish:
        runs-on: ubuntu-latest
        steps:
          - uses: actions/checkout@v3
          - uses: oven-sh/setup-bun@v1
          
          - name: Install dependencies
            run: bun install
          
          - name: Build
            run: bun run build
          
          - name: Test
            run: bun test
          
          - name: Publish to npm
            run: bun publish
            env:
              NPM_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.NPM_TOKEN }}
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/publish#related-commands)

Related Commands
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`create`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/create)
    : Create a new plugin
*   [`plugins`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/plugins)
    : Manage plugins
*   [`test`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/test)
    : Test before publishing
*   [Publish a Plugin](https://docs.elizaos.ai/guides/publish-a-plugin)
    : Complete walkthrough

Was this page helpful?

YesNo

# Update Command - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#content-area)

*   Overview
    
*   Examples
    
*   How It Works
    
*   Troubleshooting
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#usage)

Usage
----------------------------------------------------------------

    elizaos update [options]
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#options)

Options
--------------------------------------------------------------------

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| `-c, --check` | Check for available updates without applying them |
| `--skip-build` | Skip building after updating |
| `--cli` | Update only the global CLI installation (without updating packages) |
| `--packages` | Update only packages (without updating the CLI) |

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#basic-update)

Basic Update

    # Update both CLI and project dependencies (default behavior)
    elizaos update
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#checking-for-updates)

Checking for Updates

    # Check for available updates without applying them
    elizaos update --check
    

_Example Output:_

    $ elizaos update --check
    
    Checking for updates...
    Current CLI version: 1.3.5
    Latest CLI version: 1.4.0
    
    elizaOS packages that can be updated:
      - @elizaos/core (1.3.0) → 1.4.0
      - @elizaos/plugin-openai (1.2.5) → 1.4.0
    
    To apply updates, run: elizaos update
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#scoped-updates)

Scoped Updates

    # Update only the global CLI
    elizaos update --cli
    
    # Update only project packages
    elizaos update --packages
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#combined-options)

Combined Options

    # Check only for CLI updates
    elizaos update --check --cli
    
    # Update packages without rebuilding afterward
    elizaos update --packages --skip-build
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#update-process-explained)

Update Process Explained
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When you run `elizaos update`, it performs the following steps:

1.  **Detects Project Type**: Determines if you’re in an elizaOS project or plugin.
2.  **Checks CLI Version**: Compares your installed CLI version with the latest available on npm.
3.  **Scans Dependencies**: Finds all `@elizaos/*` packages in your project’s `package.json`.
4.  **Shows Update Plan**: Lists the packages and/or CLI that have available updates.
5.  **Prompts for Confirmation**: Asks for your approval before making any changes.
6.  **Updates Packages**: Installs the latest versions of the packages.
7.  **Rebuilds Project**: Compiles the updated dependencies (unless `--skip-build` is used).

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#workspace-&-monorepo-support)

Workspace & Monorepo Support

The update command is smart enough to detect monorepo workspaces. It will automatically skip any packages that are linked via `workspace:*` in your `package.json`, as these should be managed within the monorepo, not from the npm registry.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#best-practices)

Best Practices
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#safe-update-process)

Safe Update Process

For the smoothest update experience, follow this sequence:

1.  **Check what will be updated**: `elizaos update --check`
2.  **Commit your current work**: `git commit -am "chore: pre-update savepoint"`
3.  **Update the CLI first**: `elizaos update --cli`
4.  **Then, update project packages**: `elizaos update --packages`
5.  **Test your project thoroughly**: `elizaos test`

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#project-detection)

Project Detection
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The update command automatically detects:

*   **elizaOS Projects**: Updates project dependencies and rebuilds
*   **elizaOS Plugins**: Updates plugin dependencies and rebuilds
*   **Non-elizaOS Projects**: Shows error message

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#workspace-support)

Workspace Support
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#monorepo-detection)

Monorepo Detection

*   Automatically detects workspace references
*   Skips packages with `workspace:*` versions
*   Shows which packages are workspace-managed

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#example-with-workspaces)

Example with Workspaces

    $ elizaos update --check
    
    elizaOS packages found:
      - @elizaos/core (workspace:*) → Skipped (workspace reference)
      - @elizaos/plugin-openai (1.2.5) → 1.4.0
      - @elizaos/plugin-discord (workspace:*) → Skipped (workspace reference)
    
    Only non-workspace packages will be updated.
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#version-strategy)

Version Strategy
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#staying-current)

Staying Current

*   Update regularly to get latest features and fixes
*   Use `--check` to monitor available updates
*   Subscribe to elizaOS release notes

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#stability-considerations)

Stability Considerations

*   Test updates in development before production
*   Consider pinning versions for production deployments
*   Review changelogs for breaking changes

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#troubleshooting)

Troubleshooting
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#cli-update-issues)

CLI Update Issues

If you have trouble updating the global CLI:

    # Check if the CLI is installed globally
    bun pm ls -g @elizaos/cli
    
    # If not, install it
    bun install -g @elizaos/cli
    
    # On macOS/Linux, you may need sudo
    sudo bun install -g @elizaos/cli
    # Or fix permissions on your bun directory
    sudo chown -R $(whoami) ~/.bun
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#package-update-failures)

Package Update Failures

If package updates fail, a clean reinstall usually fixes it:

    # Clear caches and old dependencies
    rm -rf node_modules
    bun pm cache rm
    rm bun.lockb
    
    # Reinstall everything
    bun install
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#build-failures-after-update)

Build Failures After Update

If your project fails to build after an update:

    # Try a clean build
    bun run build
    
    # Or try updating without the build step, then build manually
    elizaos update --skip-build
    bun install && bun run build
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#version-mismatch-issues)

Version Mismatch Issues

    # Check current versions
    elizaos --version  # CLI version
    cat package.json | grep "@elizaos"  # Package versions
    
    # Force specific versions if needed
    bun add @elizaos/core@1.4.0 @elizaos/plugin-openai@1.4.0
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#network-issues)

Network Issues

    # If updates fail due to network
    # Check npm registry
    bun config get registry
    
    # Reset to default if needed
    bun config set registry https://registry.npmjs.org/
    
    # Retry update
    elizaos update
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#monorepo-update-issues)

Monorepo Update Issues

    # In monorepo, update workspace packages manually
    cd packages/core
    bun update
    
    # Or update all workspaces
    bun update --filter '*'
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/update#related-commands)

Related Commands
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [`create`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/create)
    : Create new projects with latest versions
*   [`start`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/start)
    : Start your updated project
*   [`dev`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/dev)
    : Run in development mode after updates
*   [`test`](https://docs.elizaos.ai/cli-reference/test)
    : Test your project after updates

Was this page helpful?

YesNo

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/jobs-health#content-area)

Jobs Health Check

cURL

    curl --request GET \
      --url https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health

    import requests
    
    url = "https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health"
    
    response = requests.get(url)
    
    print(response.text)

    const options = {method: 'GET'};
    
    fetch('https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health', options)
      .then(res => res.json())
      .then(res => console.log(res))
      .catch(err => console.error(err));

    <?php
    
    $curl = curl_init();
    
    curl_setopt_array($curl, [\
      CURLOPT_URL => "https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health",\
      CURLOPT_RETURNTRANSFER => true,\
      CURLOPT_ENCODING => "",\
      CURLOPT_MAXREDIRS => 10,\
      CURLOPT_TIMEOUT => 30,\
      CURLOPT_HTTP_VERSION => CURL_HTTP_VERSION_1_1,\
      CURLOPT_CUSTOMREQUEST => "GET",\
    ]);
    
    $response = curl_exec($curl);
    $err = curl_error($curl);
    
    curl_close($curl);
    
    if ($err) {
      echo "cURL Error #:" . $err;
    } else {
      echo $response;
    }

    package main
    
    import (
    	"fmt"
    	"net/http"
    	"io"
    )
    
    func main() {
    
    	url := "https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health"
    
    	req, _ := http.NewRequest("GET", url, nil)
    
    	res, _ := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
    
    	defer res.Body.Close()
    	body, _ := io.ReadAll(res.Body)
    
    	fmt.Println(string(body))
    
    }

    HttpResponse<String> response = Unirest.get("https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health")
      .asString();

    require 'uri'
    require 'net/http'
    
    url = URI("https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/health")
    
    http = Net::HTTP.new(url.host, url.port)
    http.use_ssl = true
    
    request = Net::HTTP::Get.new(url)
    
    response = http.request(request)
    puts response.read_body

{
      "status": "healthy",
      "queueSize": 5,
      "processing": 2,
      "avgProcessingTime": 1250,
      "failureRate": 0.5
    }

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/rooms/update-a-room#content-area)

Update a room

cURL

    curl --request PATCH \
      --url http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId} \
      --header 'Content-Type: application/json' \
      --data '
    {
      "name": "<string>"
    }
    '

    import requests
    
    url = "http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId}"
    
    payload = { "name": "<string>" }
    headers = {"Content-Type": "application/json"}
    
    response = requests.patch(url, json=payload, headers=headers)
    
    print(response.text)

    const options = {
      method: 'PATCH',
      headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
      body: JSON.stringify({name: '<string>'})
    };
    
    fetch('http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId}', options)
      .then(res => res.json())
      .then(res => console.log(res))
      .catch(err => console.error(err));

    <?php
    
    $curl = curl_init();
    
    curl_setopt_array($curl, [\
      CURLOPT_PORT => "3000",\
      CURLOPT_URL => "http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId}",\
      CURLOPT_RETURNTRANSFER => true,\
      CURLOPT_ENCODING => "",\
      CURLOPT_MAXREDIRS => 10,\
      CURLOPT_TIMEOUT => 30,\
      CURLOPT_HTTP_VERSION => CURL_HTTP_VERSION_1_1,\
      CURLOPT_CUSTOMREQUEST => "PATCH",\
      CURLOPT_POSTFIELDS => json_encode([\
        'name' => '<string>'\
      ]),\
      CURLOPT_HTTPHEADER => [\
        "Content-Type: application/json"\
      ],\
    ]);
    
    $response = curl_exec($curl);
    $err = curl_error($curl);
    
    curl_close($curl);
    
    if ($err) {
      echo "cURL Error #:" . $err;
    } else {
      echo $response;
    }

    package main
    
    import (
    	"fmt"
    	"strings"
    	"net/http"
    	"io"
    )
    
    func main() {
    
    	url := "http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId}"
    
    	payload := strings.NewReader("{\n  \"name\": \"<string>\"\n}")
    
    	req, _ := http.NewRequest("PATCH", url, payload)
    
    	req.Header.Add("Content-Type", "application/json")
    
    	res, _ := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
    
    	defer res.Body.Close()
    	body, _ := io.ReadAll(res.Body)
    
    	fmt.Println(string(body))
    
    }

    HttpResponse<String> response = Unirest.patch("http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId}")
      .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
      .body("{\n  \"name\": \"<string>\"\n}")
      .asString();

    require 'uri'
    require 'net/http'
    
    url = URI("http://localhost:3000/api/agents/{agentId}/rooms/{roomId}")
    
    http = Net::HTTP.new(url.host, url.port)
    
    request = Net::HTTP::Patch.new(url)
    request["Content-Type"] = 'application/json'
    request.body = "{\n  \"name\": \"<string>\"\n}"
    
    response = http.request(request)
    puts response.read_body

# Schema Management - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
> 
> Fetch the complete documentation index at: [/llms.txt](https://docs.elizaos.ai/llms.txt)
> 
> Use this file to discover all available pages before exploring further.

[Skip to main content](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#content-area)

The SQL plugin provides a sophisticated dynamic migration system that automatically manages database schemas for plugins. This guide covers how the system works and how to define schemas for your plugins.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#dynamic-migration-system)

Dynamic Migration System
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The SQL plugin uses a **dynamic migration service** that automatically creates and updates database tables based on plugin schemas. This eliminates the need for traditional migration files.

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#how-it-works)

How It Works

1.  **Plugin Registration** - Plugins export their schema definitions
2.  **Schema Discovery** - The migration service discovers all plugin schemas at startup
3.  **Schema Introspection** - The system analyzes existing database tables
4.  **Dynamic Migration** - Tables are created or updated as needed
5.  **Dependency Resolution** - Tables are created in the correct order based on foreign key dependencies

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#key-components)

Key Components

    // DatabaseMigrationService - Manages all plugin migrations
    export class DatabaseMigrationService {
      private registeredSchemas = new Map<string, any>();
    
      discoverAndRegisterPluginSchemas(plugins: Plugin[]): void {
        for (const plugin of plugins) {
          if (plugin.schema) {
            this.registeredSchemas.set(plugin.name, plugin.schema);
          }
        }
      }
    
      async runAllPluginMigrations(): Promise<void> {
        for (const [pluginName, schema] of this.registeredSchemas) {
          await runPluginMigrations(this.db!, pluginName, schema);
        }
      }
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#defining-plugin-schemas)

Defining Plugin Schemas
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To enable automatic schema management, plugins must export their Drizzle schema definitions:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#plugin-structure)

Plugin Structure

    import { Plugin } from '@elizaos/core';
    import { pgTable, uuid, text, timestamp, jsonb } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core';
    
    // Define your schema
    export const myTable = pgTable('my_table', {
      id: uuid('id').primaryKey().defaultRandom(),
      name: text('name').notNull(),
      metadata: jsonb('metadata').default(sql`'{}'::jsonb`),
      createdAt: timestamp('created_at').defaultNow(),
    });
    
    // Export as part of plugin
    export const myPlugin: Plugin = {
      name: 'my-plugin',
      schema: {
        myTable,
        // Add other tables here
      },
      // ... other plugin properties
    };
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#core-schema-tables)

Core Schema Tables
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The SQL plugin provides these core tables that all agents use:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#agent-tables)

Agent Tables

*   `agents` - Core agent identity
*   `memories` - Agent memory storage
*   `entities` - People, objects, and concepts
*   `relationships` - Connections between entities

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#communication-tables)

Communication Tables

*   `rooms` - Conversation contexts
*   `participants` - Room membership
*   `messages` - Message history

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#system-tables)

System Tables

*   `logs` - System event logging
*   `cache` - Key-value cache with composite primary key
*   `tasks` - Background task management
*   `embeddings` - Vector embeddings for similarity search

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#schema-introspection)

Schema Introspection
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The system uses `DrizzleSchemaIntrospector` to analyze database schemas:

    export class DrizzleSchemaIntrospector {
      parseTableDefinition(table: any, exportKey?: string): TableDefinition {
        const columns = this.parseColumns(table);
        const foreignKeys = this.parseForeignKeys(table);
        const indexes = this.parseIndexes(table);
        const checkConstraints = this.parseCheckConstraints(table);
        const compositePrimaryKey = this.parseCompositePrimaryKey(table);
        
        return {
          name: tableName,
          columns,
          indexes,
          foreignKeys,
          checkConstraints,
          compositePrimaryKey,
          dependencies, // Tables this table depends on
        };
      }
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#migration-process)

Migration Process
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The dynamic migrator handles various scenarios:

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#table-creation)

Table Creation

    // Automatically generates CREATE TABLE statements
    await createTable(db, tableDefinition);
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#column-addition)

Column Addition

    // Detects and adds missing columns
    if (!existingColumns.has(column.name)) {
      await addColumn(db, tableName, column);
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#index-management)

Index Management

    // Creates missing indexes
    for (const index of tableDefinition.indexes) {
      if (!existingIndexes.has(index.name)) {
        await createIndex(db, tableName, index);
      }
    }
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#foreign-key-constraints)

Foreign Key Constraints

    // Adds foreign keys after all tables exist
    for (const fk of tableDefinition.foreignKeys) {
      await addForeignKey(db, tableName, fk);
    }
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#best-practices)

Best Practices
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#1-schema-design)

1\. Schema Design

*   Use UUIDs for primary keys
*   Include timestamps (created\_at, updated\_at)
*   Use JSONB for flexible metadata
*   Define proper indexes for query performance

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#2-foreign-keys)

2\. Foreign Keys

*   Always reference existing tables
*   Consider cascade options carefully
*   Be aware of circular dependencies

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#3-composite-keys)

3\. Composite Keys

    // Example: Cache table with composite primary key
    export const cacheTable = pgTable('cache', {
      key: text('key').notNull(),
      agentId: uuid('agent_id').notNull(),
      value: jsonb('value').notNull(),
      createdAt: timestamp('created_at').defaultNow(),
    }, (table) => {
      return {
        pk: primaryKey(table.key, table.agentId),
      };
    });
    

### 

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#4-plugin-schema-organization)

4\. Plugin Schema Organization

    // Organize related tables together
    export const schema = {
      // Core tables
      users: userTable,
      profiles: profileTable,
      
      // Feature tables
      posts: postTable,
      comments: commentTable,
      
      // Junction tables
      userFollows: userFollowsTable,
    };
    

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#error-handling)

Error Handling
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The migration system includes robust error handling:

*   **Duplicate Tables** - Silently skipped
*   **Missing Dependencies** - Tables created in dependency order
*   **Failed Migrations** - Detailed error logging with rollback
*   **Schema Conflicts** - Clear error messages for debugging

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#limitations-and-considerations)

Limitations and Considerations
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1.  **No Downgrades** - The system only adds, never removes
2.  **Column Type Changes** - Not automatically handled
3.  **Data Migrations** - Must be handled separately
4.  **Production Use** - Test thoroughly before deploying schema changes

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/plugin-registry/sql/schema-management#example-complete-plugin-schema)

Example: Complete Plugin Schema
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    import { Plugin } from '@elizaos/core';
    import { pgTable, uuid, text, timestamp, jsonb, boolean, integer } from 'drizzle-orm/pg-core';
    import { sql } from 'drizzle-orm';
    
    // Define tables
    export const projectTable = pgTable('projects', {
      id: uuid('id').primaryKey().defaultRandom(),
      agentId: uuid('agent_id').notNull(),
      name: text('name').notNull(),
      description: text('description'),
      status: text('status').default('active'),
      metadata: jsonb('metadata').default(sql`'{}'::jsonb`),
      createdAt: timestamp('created_at').defaultNow(),
      updatedAt: timestamp('updated_at').defaultNow(),
    });
    
    export const taskTable = pgTable('project_tasks', {
      id: uuid('id').primaryKey().defaultRandom(),
      projectId: uuid('project_id').notNull().references(() => projectTable.id),
      title: text('title').notNull(),
      completed: boolean('completed').default(false),
      priority: integer('priority').default(0),
      dueDate: timestamp('due_date'),
      createdAt: timestamp('created_at').defaultNow(),
    });
    
    // Create indexes
    export const projectIndexes = {
      agentIdIdx: index('project_agent_id_idx').on(projectTable.agentId),
      statusIdx: index('project_status_idx').on(projectTable.status),
    };
    
    // Export plugin
    export const projectPlugin: Plugin = {
      name: 'project-management',
      schema: {
        projectTable,
        taskTable,
        ...projectIndexes,
      },
      // ... other plugin properties
    };
    

This schema will be automatically created when the agent starts, with all tables, columns, indexes, and foreign keys properly configured.

cURL

    curl --request GET \

    import requests
    
    url = "https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/{jobId}"
    
    response = requests.get(url)
    
    print(response.text)

    const options = {method: 'GET'};
    
    fetch('https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/{jobId}', options)
      .then(res => res.json())
      .then(res => console.log(res))
      .catch(err => console.error(err));

    <?php

    curl_setopt_array($curl, [\
      CURLOPT_URL => "https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/{jobId}",\
      CURLOPT_RETURNTRANSFER => true,\
      CURLOPT_ENCODING => "",\
      CURLOPT_MAXREDIRS => 10,\
      CURLOPT_TIMEOUT => 30,\
      CURLOPT_HTTP_VERSION => CURL_HTTP_VERSION_1_1,\
      CURLOPT_CUSTOMREQUEST => "GET",\
    ]);
    
    $response = curl_exec($curl);
    $err = curl_error($curl);
    
    curl_close($curl);
    
    if ($err) {
      echo "cURL Error #:" . $err;
    } else {
      echo $response;
    }

    package main

    	"fmt"
    	"net/http"
    	"io"
    )

    	url := "https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/{jobId}"
    
    	req, _ := http.NewRequest("GET", url, nil)
    
    	res, _ := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
    
    	defer res.Body.Close()
    	body, _ := io.ReadAll(res.Body)
    
    	fmt.Println(string(body))
    
    }

    HttpResponse<String> response = Unirest.get("https://api.example.com/api/messaging/jobs/{jobId}")
      .asString();

    require 'uri'

    http = Net::HTTP.new(url.host, url.port)
    http.use_ssl = true
    
    request = Net::HTTP::Get.new(url)
    
    response = http.request(request)
    puts response.read_body

Get the status and result of a messaging job.

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#path-parameters)

Path Parameters
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-job-id)

jobId

string

required

The job ID returned from job creation

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#response)

Response
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-job-id-1)

jobId

string

Unique job identifier

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-status)

status

string

Job status: `queued`, `processing`, `completed`, `failed`, `timeout`

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-created-at)

createdAt

number

Unix timestamp of job creation

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-started-at)

startedAt

number

Unix timestamp when processing started

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-completed-at)

completedAt

number

Unix timestamp when job completed

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-result)

result

object

Job result (only present when status is `completed`)

Show Result object

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-text)

text

string

Agent response text

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-actions)

actions

array

Actions executed

[​](https://docs.elizaos.ai/rest-reference/jobs/get-job#param-error)

error

string

# Create a room - ElizaOS Documentation

> Documentation Index
> -------------------
>

>
